Confirmed against the old `boundedFile`: with this fake change alone (before the field rename), `TestFetchMedia_CapsAFileStreamLargerThanDeclared` and `TestFetchMedia_CapsAnImageStreamWithNoDeclaredSize` failed, oversized data sailing straight through; both pass again once `boundedFile` stopped embedding.

**Telegram's own streamed download was already safe.** gotd's `downloader.Builder.Stream` takes a statically-typed `io.Writer` and its internal `writeLoop` calls `w.Write(part.data)` directly - never `io.Copy`, so there is no `ReadFrom`/`WriteTo` promotion to bypass in the first place. `connector.BoundedWriter`, which Telegram's `download` wraps the destination file in, is a plain struct that embeds nothing, so even a future call path that did use `io.Copy` would find no promoted method to exploit. No change was needed there; `fetch_limits_test.go`'s `TestFetchMedia_CapsADocumentStreamLargerThanDeclared` already drives the real gotd downloader end to end, not a hand-rolled stand-in, so it would have caught the same class of bug had one existed.

## Extra `0xFF` fill bytes before a JPEG marker bypassed the scan and table caps

A follow-up security review found that `imagesafe/jpeg.go`'s `copyJPEGRemainder` - added to bound a progressive JPEG's own repeated scans, Huffman and quantization tables (see the entry above it) - classified whatever byte sat right after the *first* `0xFF` it found as the marker code. The JPEG spec allows any number of `0xFF` fill bytes to precede a real marker, and libjpeg-turbo's own `next_marker` skips all of them before reading the marker code; this package's own walk did not, so `FF FF DA` was read as marker `0xFF` (not a marker this package recognises) followed by a fresh search starting at `DA`, and the start-of-scan the fill byte was hiding was never counted at all. The same hole let a repeated DHT or DQT segment back past `jpegMaxRepeatedTableSegments`. This was the exact attack the scan cap was meant to close, just with one fill byte added back in front of each repeat.

**The fix.** `copyJPEGRemainder` now skips a run of `0xFF` fill bytes the same way `next_marker` does, after writing the first one through but before classifying the byte that follows as a marker: whatever comes after the run is what gets compared against `0xD9`/`0xDA`/`0xC4`/`0xDB` and counted, regardless of how many fill bytes sat in front of it. The run itself is collapsed in the output down to that single leading `0xFF` - fill bytes carry no meaning of their own, so a decoder reads the collapsed form identically, and a long run can no longer be used to push a marker out of where a naive scan would expect to find it. A new `jpegMaxFillBytes` (64 KiB, counted across the whole walk, not per run) bounds the total skipped even when no single run or marker count on its own would trip `jpegMaxScans` or `jpegMaxRepeatedTableSegments` - the same "bound a count no real encoder would ever produce" reasoning the other two caps already use, since a real encoder has no reason to pad with anything beyond a handful of bytes.

**Tests.** `insertFakeMarkersWithFill` (`helpers_test.go`) builds the exact bypass shape - `extraFill` additional `0xFF` bytes before a chosen marker, repeated `count` times - reusing `insertFakeMarkers`' own trick of needing only the two marker bytes, not a fully-formed segment, to drive the counts. `TestSanitizeJPEG_CountsMarkersPastFillBytes` reproduces the report's bypass for SOS, DHT and DQT with one extra fill byte each, repeated two million times, and confirms rejection well under a second - run against the pre-fix `copyJPEGRemainder`, this failed exactly as the review described, bytes sailing through uncounted. `TestSanitizeJPEG_CollapsesOrdinaryFillBytes` confirms a small, ordinary fill run is still accepted, with the run actually collapsed in the output rather than just tolerated. `TestSanitizeJPEG_CapsTotalFillBytes` covers a single fill run past `jpegMaxFillBytes` with too few markers to trip the other two caps on its own. `FuzzSanitizeJPEG` gained seeds at this exact shape, past `jpegMaxScans` and `jpegMaxRepeatedTableSegments` with fill bytes added, and past `jpegMaxFillBytes` alone.

## A saved file's own name could still carry markup characters

A regression review found that `app/mediasave.go`'s `sanitizedStem` stripped control and bidi/format characters from a sender's claimed file name, but still kept `<`, `>`, `&`, `=`, `#`, `;` and other punctuation outside any allow-list. Qt's own non-native `FileDialog` (`options: FileDialog.DontUseNativeDialog`, used by `Composer.qml`'s attach picker and `ListController.qml`'s notification-sound picker whenever the portal's own chooser is unavailable) lists each file through `FileDialogDelegateLabel.qml`'s plain `Text { text: root.delegate.fileName }`, with no `textFormat` set - every Qt Quick Controls style defaults that to `AutoText`, which renders a name containing `<img src=...>` as rich text and fetches whatever it points at just by listing the folder. A document named `<img src=https:&#47;&#47;evil.example&#47;t.png>.pdf` - Qt's own HTML parser decodes the `&#47;` entities even though a real file name can never contain a literal `/` - would leak the user's IP address and a timing signal to the sender the moment they browsed into Downloads for an unrelated reason, with no click on the message itself.

**The fix.** `sanitizedStem` now runs a conservative allow-list, `restrictStemChars`, after collapsing whitespace: letters, digits, space and a fixed set of punctuation (`- _ . ( ) , +`) pass through unchanged, and everything else - markup characters included - becomes `_`. This runs in the one place every saved name already passes through, so it covers every sender and every connector without touching `ui/` at all.

**The fallback dialogs' own starting folder was not changed.** The regression review's general shape of a fix also suggested the fallback dialogs stop relying on Qt's own rendering of a foreign file name altogether - for instance, by never letting them start browsing inside Downloads. Neither `Composer.qml`'s attach `FileDialog` nor `ListController.qml`'s sound `FileDialog` sets a `currentFolder` today, so neither already points at Downloads specifically; a user who wants to attach or pick a sound they just received would have to navigate there themselves either way. Once a saved name can no longer carry a character `AutoText` reads as markup, there is nothing left in Downloads for either dialog's label to misread, so steering their starting folder away from it would add complexity (a `StandardPaths` lookup, a decision about where to point instead) without closing a gap the character fix had not already closed. Left as is unless a future review finds another reason to change it.

**Tests.** `TestSaveMedia_NeverWritesMarkupCharactersInTheFileName` (`mediasave_test.go`) covers the report's own scenario, a sender-claimed name built around `<img src=x>` alongside `&`, `#` and `;`, and asserts none of those six characters ever reach the saved file's own name - confirmed to fail against the pre-fix `sanitizedStem` before `restrictStemChars` was added.

## A second quadratic trailing-character regex, in `Links.js`'s own copy, plus two more of the same shape found by sweeping the rest of `ui/lib`

A security review found `Links.js`'s `normalizeTarget` trimmed a link's own trailing slash with `/\/+$/` - a quantified character class anchored only at the end, not the start. With no start anchor, a regex engine retries the match from every position in the string, and at each one inside a long run of "/" it backtracks the `+` from its greedy maximum back down to one before giving up - costing the square of the run's length, not its length. `sameTarget` calls this twice on every link open (the click path and the "o" key, each normalizing both the link's own visible text and its href), so `https://a` followed by about 65,000 "/" and then "b" cost roughly 2×10⁹ backtracking steps per call - the exact shape `Format.splitTrailingPunctuation` and `Highlight.stripTrailingPunctuation` were already rewritten as backward scans to close, in `ui/lib`'s other two link-handling files, when this same review previously found it there; this copy, added later in `Links.js`, was missed.

**The fix.** `normalizeTarget` now calls `Links.trimTrailingSlashes`, a backward scan from the string's own end - linear by construction, since there is no quantifier ambiguity left for anything to backtrack.

**The sweep.** Fixing a regex found this way by inspection alone risks missing a sibling copy, so every regex literal and `RegExp` construction in `ui/lib`, `ui/components` and `ui/controllers` was listed and checked for the same shape: an unanchored or globally-reapplied quantifier whose character class can run to the end of the input with nothing to stop it early. Most are either anchored at both ends (so only one start position is ever tried), or already excluded their own terminator from an adjoining character class AND have a terminator that is guaranteed nearby - `Format.URL_PATTERN`'s HTML-entity suffix, for instance, where `[^\s<"&]*` can only stop at a `&` that is itself followed by one of a handful of fixed-length entities, each closed with its own `;` within a few characters. Two more did not have that guarantee once fed adversarial input directly, confirmed by writing the same timed, 65,000-character adversarial case for every regex-bearing exported function in the sweep (`tests/unit/redos-bounds.test.cjs`) rather than trusting the reasoning alone:

- `Format.highlight`'s own `.split(/(&[^;]*;)/)`, meant to keep an HTML entity opaque while highlighting a search match around it, excludes `;` from its class the same correct way, but a run of `&` with no `;` anywhere after it - not reachable through `highlight`'s one real caller today, which always pre-escapes its text through `escapeHtml`, but reachable by calling the function directly - backtracks that class to zero at every `&` in the run, the identical shape. Replaced with `Format.splitEntities`, a forward scan that finds each entity's own next `;` directly rather than letting the regex engine search for it by trial and error.
- `Format.initials`'s own `.replace(/\([^)]*\)/g, ' ')`, stripping a bracketed aside such as "(Manager)" from a display name before picking initials, has the same shape: a run of `(` with no `)` anywhere after it. A display name is capped server-side at `domain.MaxDisplayNameLength` (256) today, so the practical cost through `Avatar.qml`'s one real caller is trivial, but the function itself had no bound of its own. Replaced with `Format.stripAsides`, the same kind of forward scan.

Both replacements preserve the exact split or substitution the regex produced, including the "no terminator found at all" case: the original regex simply fails to match from that point on, leaving the remainder of the text untouched, which each new loop does explicitly by copying the rest through unchanged rather than continuing to scan for a terminator that cannot exist.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/links.test.cjs` gained the same adversarial case `tests/unit/format.test.cjs` and `tests/unit/highlight.test.cjs` already carry for their own trailing-punctuation fix, now for `sameTarget`'s trailing-slash one: a 65,000-character run, asserting both the real result and a two-second bound. `tests/unit/redos-bounds.test.cjs` is the sweep's own record, independent of any single lib file: one bounded, adversarial case per regex-bearing exported function the sweep covered, each fed 65,000 of its own regex's boundary character, so a future change that reintroduces this shape - in one of these functions or a new one added the same way - fails the build on its own rather than waiting for another review to notice by inspection.

## A real GIF video's AnimatedImage still sized its decode from the sender's claimed width/height, not the file's own verified canvas

A security review found that `InlineVideo.qml`'s and `FullVideoPlayer.qml`'s own real-GIF `AnimatedImage` - the one case where a video's full, downloaded file (never a thumbnail) decodes through an image engine at all, once `decodeAs` confirms it is a genuine GIF - called `ImageBounds.boundedSourceSize` with `root.video.width`/`root.video.height`: the sender's own claimed video dimensions, read from the message's metadata, with nothing bounding them server-side and nothing checking them against the real file. Every other sink `boundedSourceSize` ever shrinks a decode for was already fixed to use a verified size instead ("`ImageBounds.boundedSourceSize`'s own fix still read a sender's claimed width and height, not the real file's", above); a line this file's own earlier comment added - "A video's full file is never shown through a plain Image at all - only AnimatedImage, measured not to amplify a mismatch this way" - never actually measured that claim, and this is the counterexample: `boundedSourceSize`'s own math is correct relative to the *box*, but a box of, say, 1400x1 (what a 2000000x1 claim scales down to inside a 1400x860 bubble) still makes Qt's own covering scale read the *real* file's wildly different ratio (a 128x2048 GIF, say) and amplify past it - exactly the decode-bomb shape every other fix in this section closes, just reached through a video's claimed size rather than a photo's.

**Why nothing caught this earlier.** `servedPath` (`app.Commands`) only runs `connector.EnsureImageSafe` for a photo or sticker, so `media.fetch` never returned a verified size for a video at all; `connector.SanitizeIfGIF` sanitized a real GIF video's bytes through `imagesafe.AnimatedGIF` but discarded the width and height that sanitizer already parsed. There was nothing for `ImageBounds.verifiedDimensions` to read for a video even if the UI had called it.

**The fix, three layers.**
- `connector.SanitizeIfGIF` now returns the GIF's own real width and height alongside `isGIF`, from the same `imagesafe.Sanitize` call it already makes.
- `app.Commands.DecodeAsForMedia` returns them too, and `server.mediaFetch` (`methods.go`) uses them in place of `FetchMedia`'s own (always zero, for a video) width/height whenever `decodeAs` comes back `"gif"` - the same additive `width`/`height` fields `media.fetch`'s result already carries for a photo or sticker, now also set for this one video case (`docs/api.md`).
- `MediaController._applyVerifiedSize` needed no change at all: it already records any non-zero `result.width`/`result.height` as `verifiedWidth`/`verifiedHeight` on the message's own media, whatever kind that media is. `InlineVideo.qml`'s and `FullVideoPlayer.qml`'s real-GIF `AnimatedImage` now read `ImageBounds.verifiedDimensions(video, true)` the same way `PhotoView`/`PhotoViewer`/`StickerView` already do, falling back to the box - never the claim - before that answer exists.

**Defence in depth: `domain.CapMedia` now bounds `Media.Width`/`Height` too**, at a new `MaxMediaDimension` (65536px), the same belt-and-suspenders reasoning `ImageBounds.boundedSourceSize` itself already gets even once every real sink reads a verified size: cheap insurance for any sourceSize calculation that ever again reads this claim directly, in this file or a new one, without ever becoming the actual fix - the real fix is the UI never reading the claim for sizing a decode at all.

**Tests.** `backend/internal/connector/media_test.go` and `backend/internal/app/media_decode_test.go` cover `SanitizeIfGIF`'s and `DecodeAsForMedia`'s new width/height return values, the app-level one with a message carrying a 2000000x1 claim to confirm the real, decoded 1x1 canvas is what comes back, never the claim. `backend/internal/server/server_test.go`'s `TestMediaFetch_ReportsVerifiedDimensionsForARealGIFVideo` covers the same thing through `media.fetch` end to end. `backend/internal/domain/limits_test.go` covers `CapMedia`'s new width/height clamp. `tests/unit/image-bounds.test.cjs` covers `verifiedDimensions`/`boundedSourceSize` together producing the box fallback before a verified size exists and the real, small size once it does, for the exact 2000000x1-claim shape. `tests/qml/InlineVideo/shell.qml` and `tests/qml/PhotoViewer/shell.qml` each gained a case recomputing `boundedSourceSize` from a verified 4x3 over whatever box the component's own (lying-aspect-ratio-driven) layout settled on, and requiring the component's own `AnimatedImage.sourceSize` to match it exactly - the same pattern `PhotoView`'s own lying-dimensions case already used for a photo.

## `Media.ThumbWidth`/`ThumbHeight` were only ever computed by a read, so a live, backfilled or edited message reached the UI without them

A security review found that `store.sanitizedThumb` - the function that turns a thumbnail's own bytes into `ThumbWidth`/`ThumbHeight` - ran only inside `scanMessage`'s own `decodeMessageColumns`, which fires when a row is read back out of the database with `Message`, `MessageByRemote` or `Messages`. Nothing called it on the write side: `addMessage` returned the connector's own message (`m`, built straight from its argument) with no read in between, and `EditMessage` built its return value from `edit.Media` the same way. `app.Ingest.Incoming`, `.History` and `.Edited` all publish that returned value directly as `message.added` or `message.updated`, with no extra read of the row. So a message delivered live, backfilled from history, or edited to attach new media reached the UI with `ThumbWidth`/`ThumbHeight` still zero, and `ImageBounds.verifiedDimensions` then fell back to the display box alone for `sourceSize` - the same decode-bomb shape the box-alone fallback was already shown unsafe against elsewhere in this file, now reachable again through any of these three paths, including re-arming an older photo already loaded with its correct dimensions by editing it.

**The fix.** `store.verifyThumb(media *domain.Media) *domain.Media` wraps `sanitizedThumb` for a `*domain.Media` whole, returning a copy with `Thumb`, `ThumbWidth` and `ThumbHeight` all freshly computed, or `media` unchanged when it is nil or carries no thumbnail. `addMessage` calls it on `m.Media` right after assigning defaults, before the row is ever inserted; `fillMedia` (a message stored before the service reported media for it, filled in later) calls it on its own `media` argument before storing; `EditMessage` calls it on `edit.Media` before storing and before building its return value. The message every one of these three functions returns - and so what `Ingest` publishes - now carries exactly the same `ThumbWidth`/`ThumbHeight` a subsequent read of the same row would compute, because both paths now call the one function. This is not a new dependency: `store` already imports `imagesafe` for `sanitizedThumb` itself.

**Why in `store`, not `app` or the connectors.** The connectors already run a thumbnail through `imagesafe.Sanitize` once (WhatsApp's `thumb`, Telegram's `strippedThumb`), but both deliberately discard the width and height that call returns, reasoning (now outdated) that `store.sanitizedThumb` would recompute them on every read regardless. Threading the connectors' own already-verified width and height through `mediaRef`/`domain.Media` all the way to `store` would mean two separate, divergent paths for the exact same number; calling `verifyThumb` in `store` instead keeps the one place `ThumbWidth`/`ThumbHeight` are ever computed exactly one place, for a write the same way it already was for a read, at the cost of one extra, cheap re-parse of a thumbnail already capped to `imagesafe.Thumbnail`'s own small size.

**Tests.** `backend/internal/store/thumbsafe_test.go` gained `TestAddMessage_ReturnsVerifiedThumbDimensions` and `TestEditMessage_ReturnsVerifiedThumbDimensions`, each asserting the dimensions on the function's own return value, with no intervening read. `backend/internal/app/ingest_test.go` gained `TestIncoming_PublishesVerifiedThumbDimensions`, `TestHistory_PublishesVerifiedThumbDimensions` and `TestEdited_PublishesVerifiedThumbDimensions`, each reading the data `fakePublisher` recorded for the matching event and confirming it already carries the thumbnail's real 8x8 size over a sender's claimed 60000x1, all four failing against the pre-fix code for the expected reason (zero width and height) before `verifyThumb` existed.
